## 1:1 Checkpoint-to-Commit Mapping with Deferred Transcript Finalization

### Problem

Previously, checkpoints used a 1:N model — one checkpoint ID could span multiple commits. When an agent made several commits during a single turn, they all shared the same checkpoint ID. This made it impossible to tie a specific commit to a specific snapshot of session context, and the `ACTIVE_COMMITTED` phase added
 complexity to the state machine.

### Approach

Every user commit now gets its own unique checkpoint ID. The core challenge is that mid-turn commits happen before the agent is done — the transcript is incomplete. We solve this with a **provisional-then-finalize** pattern:

1. **At commit time (PostCommit hook):** Condense whatever transcript exists so far to `entire/checkpoints/v1` with a fresh checkpoint ID. This is *provisional* — the transcript is partial. Then carry forward any remaining uncommitted files to a new shadow branch so the next commit gets its own checkpoint.

2. **At turn end (Stop hook):** `HandleTurnEnd` iterates over all checkpoint IDs recorded during the turn (`TurnCheckpointIDs` on session state) and replaces each provisional transcript with the complete one via `UpdateCommitted`.

This means every checkpoint on `entire/checkpoints/v1` ends up with the full prompt-to-stop transcript, while each commit still gets its own unique ID for precise linking.

### Key changes

**New: `UpdateCommitted` on checkpoint store**
- Replace semantics (not append) — swaps transcript, prompts, and context blobs for a checkpoint's session folder
- Chunks large transcripts (matching `WriteCommitted` behavior)
- Redacts secrets before writing (matching `WriteCommitted` behavior)

**New: `TurnCheckpointIDs` on session state**
- Tracks checkpoint IDs condensed during the current agent turn
- Populated in PostCommit for ACTIVE sessions after successful condensation
- Consumed in `HandleTurnEnd` to finalize all checkpoints
- Cleared on new prompt (both strategies)

**New: `HandleTurnEnd` finalization**
- `TurnEndHandler` interface simplified — no longer takes `[]session.Action` parameter since work items come from state, not the action list
- Called unconditionally when strategy implements the interface (removed dead `if len(remaining) > 0` guard)

**Phase simplification**
- Removed `ACTIVE_COMMITTED` phase — the state machine is now 3 phases: ACTIVE, IDLE, ENDED
- `active_committed` in persisted state normalizes to ACTIVE (not IDLE) so pending finalization isn't lost on upgrade

**PostCommit carry-forward**
- After condensation, uncommitted files are carried forward to a new shadow branch
- Ensures the next commit gets its own checkpoint ID instead of accumulating into the previous one
```

The review has been written to `/Users/alex/workspace/cli/docs/reviews/review-01.md`.

Here is a summary of the key findings:

**Verdict: APPROVE (with suggestions)**

The PR is well-designed and thoroughly tested. No critical issues were found. Here are the highlights:

**Strengths:**
- The 1:1 checkpoint model is a clean simplification over 1:N
- The provisional-then-finalize pattern is robust -- provisional data is always persisted, finalization is best-effort, so failures degrade gracefully rather than losing data
- The `TurnCheckpointIDs` lifecycle is correctly implemented across all code paths (set in PostCommit, consumed in HandleTurnEnd, cleared in HandleTurnEnd and InitializeSession)
- Content-aware overlap detection (`content_overlap.go`) prevents false checkpoint attribution when users revert and replace session files
- Comprehensive integration tests (8 scenarios) covering deferred finalization, carry-forward, overlap detection, session depletion, reverted files, and session reset
- Backward compatibility is carefully maintained via `PhaseFromString` normalization and `NormalizeAfterLoad` migration

**Notable findings:**

1. **[checkpoint.go:296]** Minor typo: stray `/` instead of `//` in comment on `UpdateCommittedOptions`

2. **[manual_commit_hooks.go:1811-1814]** The carry-forward code resets `StepCount` and `LastCheckpointID` but intentionally preserves `TurnCheckpointIDs`. Consider adding a comment clarifying this is intentional (the checkpoints from earlier in the turn still need finalization at turn end).

3. **Subtle coupling in TurnCheckpointIDs tracking** (manual_commit_hooks.go:643): The `state.Phase.IsActive()` check runs after `TransitionAndLog` updates the phase. It works because `ACTIVE + GitCommit -> ACTIVE` in the state machine, but if that transition ever changed, the check would silently fail.

4. **Double redaction** in `UpdateCommitted` (committed.go:1078-1086): The transcript is redacted in `finalizeAllTurnCheckpoints` before being passed to `UpdateCommitted`, which redacts again. Harmless but unnecessary CPU work.

5. **Concurrent metadata branch writes**: Cross-process concurrent `UpdateCommitted` calls could theoretically race, but the sequential execution model makes this extremely unlikely in practice.

The review has been written to `/Users/alex/workspace/cli/docs/requirements/one-to-one-checkpoints/review-01.md`.

Here is a summary of the findings:

**Verdict: APPROVE**

### Test Coverage -- All Key Scenarios Covered

1. **Mid-turn commit**: Unit tests in `phase_postcommit_test.go` and `mid_turn_commit_test.go`, plus the `TestShadow_DeferredTranscriptFinalization` integration test exercise the full flow.

2. **Finalization at turn end**: `HandleTurnEnd` is tested for both the no-op path (no TurnCheckpointIDs) and the active path. The `TestShadow_MultipleCommits_SameActiveTurn` integration test validates 3 commits in one turn, all finalized at stop.

3. **Carry-forward**: Both partial commit and full commit cases are covered at unit level (`phase_postcommit_test.go:927-1101`) and integration level (`deferred_finalization_test.go:241-416`).

4. **Multiple commits in one turn**: Comprehensive integration test at `deferred_finalization_test.go:428`.

5. **Content overlap detection**: 12 unit tests in `content_overlap_test.go` covering all branches of `filesOverlapWithContent`, `stagedFilesOverlapWithContent`, and `filesWithRemainingAgentChanges`. Integration tests cover the "reverted and replaced" and "unrelated commit" scenarios.

### Missing Edge Cases (Minor)

- `HandleTurnEnd` partial failure (some UpdateCommitted calls succeed, others fail)
- `HandleTurnEnd` with empty/unreadable transcript path
- Deleted file overlap detection (git rm path in `filesOverlapWithContent`)

### Test Quality

- Well-structured with descriptive doc comments on every test
- Correct `t.Parallel()` usage throughout (including properly omitting it for `t.Chdir` tests)
- Strong helper functions reduce boilerplate

### content_overlap.go Correctness

The implementation is sound with a clean two-tier design: modified files always count as overlap, new files require content match. Appropriate fail-open fallbacks when git state is unreadable. One design note: minor edits to agent-created new files prevent checkpoint linkage, but this is documented and intentional.

### Flaky Risk: Low

Tests are well-isolated using `t.TempDir()`, have no shared mutable state, and the one timing-dependent test (`time.Sleep(time.Millisecond)`) uses a tolerant assertion.

   651→		// partial changes, the file still has remaining agent changes to carry forward.

   701→// condenseAndUpdateState runs condensation for a session and updates state afterward.
   702→// Returns true if condensation succeeded.
   703→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) condenseAndUpdateState(
   704→	logCtx context.Context,
   705→	repo *git.Repository,
   706→	checkpointID id.CheckpointID,
   707→	state *SessionState,
   708→	head *plumbing.Reference,
   709→	shadowBranchName string,
   710→	shadowBranchesToDelete map[string]struct{},
   711→) bool {
   712→	result, err := s.CondenseSession(repo, checkpointID, state)
   713→	if err != nil {
   714→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: condensation failed for session %s: %v\n",
   715→			state.SessionID, err)
   716→		logging.Warn(logCtx, "post-commit: condensation failed",
   717→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   718→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   719→		)

  1590→// finalizeAllTurnCheckpoints replaces the provisional transcript in each checkpoint
  1591→// created during this turn with the full session transcript.
  1592→//
  1593→// This is called at turn end (stop hook). During the turn, PostCommit wrote whatever
  1594→// transcript was available at commit time. Now we have the complete transcript and
  1595→// replace it so every checkpoint has the full prompt-to-stop context.
  1596→//
  1597→// Returns the number of errors encountered (best-effort: continues processing on error).
  1598→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) finalizeAllTurnCheckpoints(state *SessionState) int {
  1599→	if len(state.TurnCheckpointIDs) == 0 {
  1600→		return 0 // No mid-turn commits to finalize
  1601→	}
  1602→
  1603→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
  1604→
  1605→	logging.Info(logCtx, "finalizing turn checkpoints with full transcript",
  1606→		slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
  1607→		slog.Int("checkpoint_count", len(state.TurnCheckpointIDs)),
  1608→	)
  1609→
  1610→	errCount := 0
  1611→
  1612→	// Read full transcript from live transcript file
  1613→	if state.TranscriptPath == "" {
  1614→		logging.Warn(logCtx, "finalize: no transcript path, skipping",
  1615→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
  1616→		)
  1617→		state.TurnCheckpointIDs = nil
  1618→		return 1 // Count as error - all checkpoints will be skipped
  1619→	}
  1620→
  1621→	fullTranscript, err := os.ReadFile(state.TranscriptPath)
  1622→	if err != nil || len(fullTranscript) == 0 {
  1623→		logging.Warn(logCtx, "finalize: failed to read transcript, skipping",
  1624→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
  1625→			slog.String("transcript_path", state.TranscriptPath),
  1626→		)
  1627→		state.TurnCheckpointIDs = nil
  1628→		return 1 // Count as error - all checkpoints will be skipped
  1629→	}
  1630→
  1631→	// Extract prompts and context from the full transcript
  1632→	prompts := extractUserPrompts(state.AgentType, string(fullTranscript))
  1633→	contextBytes := generateContextFromPrompts(prompts)
  1634→
  1635→	// Redact secrets before writing — matches WriteCommitted behavior.
  1636→	// The live transcript on disk contains raw content; redaction must happen
  1637→	// before anything is persisted to the metadata branch.
  1638→	fullTranscript, err = redact.JSONLBytes(fullTranscript)
  1639→	if err != nil {
  1640→		logging.Warn(logCtx, "finalize: transcript redaction failed, skipping",
  1641→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
  1642→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
  1643→		)
  1644→		state.TurnCheckpointIDs = nil
  1645→		return 1 // Count as error - all checkpoints will be skipped
  1646→	}
  1647→	for i, p := range prompts {
  1648→		prompts[i] = redact.String(p)
  1649→	}
  1650→	contextBytes = redact.Bytes(contextBytes)
  1651→
  1652→	// Open repository and create checkpoint store
  1653→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
  1654→	if err != nil {
  1655→		logging.Warn(logCtx, "finalize: failed to open repository",
  1656→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
  1657→		)
  1658→		state.TurnCheckpointIDs = nil
  1659→		return 1 // Count as error - all checkpoints will be skipped
  1660→	}
  1661→	store := checkpoint.NewGitStore(repo)
  1662→
  1663→	// Update each checkpoint with the full transcript
  1664→	for _, cpIDStr := range state.TurnCheckpointIDs {
  1665→		cpID, parseErr := id.NewCheckpointID(cpIDStr)
  1666→		if parseErr != nil {
  1667→			logging.Warn(logCtx, "finalize: invalid checkpoint ID, skipping",
  1668→				slog.String("checkpoint_id", cpIDStr),
  1669→				slog.String("error", parseErr.Error()),
  1670→			)
  1671→			errCount++
  1672→			continue
  1673→		}
  1674→
  1675→		updateErr := store.UpdateCommitted(context.Background(), checkpoint.UpdateCommittedOptions{
  1676→			CheckpointID: cpID,
  1677→			SessionID:    state.SessionID,
  1678→			Transcript:   fullTranscript,
  1679→			Prompts:      prompts,
  1680→			Context:      contextBytes,
  1681→			Agent:        state.AgentType,
  1682→		})
  1683→		if updateErr != nil {
  1684→			logging.Warn(logCtx, "finalize: failed to update checkpoint",
  1685→				slog.String("checkpoint_id", cpIDStr),
  1686→				slog.String("error", updateErr.Error()),
  1687→			)
  1688→			errCount++
  1689→			continue
  1690→		}
  1691→
  1692→		logging.Info(logCtx, "finalize: checkpoint updated with full transcript",
  1693→			slog.String("checkpoint_id", cpIDStr),
  1694→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
  1695→		)
  1696→	}
  1697→
  1698→	// Update transcript start and clear turn checkpoint IDs
  1699→	fullTranscriptLines := countTranscriptItems(state.AgentType, string(fullTranscript))
  1700→	state.CheckpointTranscriptStart = fullTranscriptLines
  1701→	state.TurnCheckpointIDs = nil
  1702→
  1703→	return errCount
  1704→}

  1707→func filesChangedInCommit(commit *object.Commit) map[string]struct{} {
  1708→	result := make(map[string]struct{})
  1709→

     8→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5"

    10→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing/object"
    11→)
    12→
    13→// Content-aware overlap detection for checkpoint management.
    14→//
    15→// These functions determine whether a commit contains session-related work by comparing
    16→// file content (not just filenames) against the shadow branch. This enables accurate
    17→// detection of the "reverted and replaced" scenario where a user:
    18→// 1. Reverts session changes (e.g., git checkout -- file.txt)
    19→// 2. Creates completely different content in the same file
    20→// 3. Commits the new content
    21→//
    22→// In this scenario, the commit should NOT get a checkpoint trailer because the
    23→// session's work was discarded, not incorporated.
    24→//
    25→// The key distinction:
    26→// - Modified files (exist in parent commit): Always count as overlap, regardless of
    27→//   content changes. The user is editing session's work.
    28→// - New files (don't exist in parent): Require content match against shadow branch.
    29→//   If content differs completely, the session's work was likely reverted & replaced.
    30→
    31→// filesOverlapWithContent checks if any file in filesTouched overlaps with the committed
    32→// content, using content-aware comparison to detect the "reverted and replaced" scenario.
    33→//
    34→// This is used in PostCommit to determine if a session has work in the commit.
    35→func filesOverlapWithContent(repo *git.Repository, shadowBranchName string, headCommit *object.Commit, filesTouched []string) bool {
    36→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
    37→
    38→	// Build set of filesTouched for quick lookup
    39→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
    40→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
    41→		touchedSet[f] = true
    42→	}
    43→
    44→	// Get HEAD commit tree (the committed content)
    45→	headTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
    46→	if err != nil {
    47→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD tree, falling back to filename check",
    48→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    49→		)
    50→		return len(filesTouched) > 0 // Fall back: assume overlap if any files touched
    51→	}
    52→
    53→	// Get shadow branch tree (the session's content)
    54→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
    55→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
    56→	if err != nil {
    57→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: shadow branch not found, falling back to filename check",
    58→			slog.String("branch", shadowBranchName),
    59→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    60→		)
    61→		return len(filesTouched) > 0 // Fall back: assume overlap if any files touched
    62→	}
    63→
    64→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
    65→	if err != nil {
    66→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get shadow commit, falling back to filename check",
    67→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    68→		)
    69→		return len(filesTouched) > 0
    70→	}
    71→
    72→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
    73→	if err != nil {
    74→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get shadow tree, falling back to filename check",
    75→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    76→		)
    77→		return len(filesTouched) > 0
    78→	}
    79→
    80→	// Get the parent commit tree to determine if files are modified vs newly created.
    81→	// For modified files (exist in parent), we count as overlap regardless of content
    82→	// because the user is editing the session's work.
    83→	// For newly created files (don't exist in parent), we check content to detect
    84→	// the "reverted and replaced" scenario where user deleted session's work and
    85→	// created something completely different.
    86→	var parentTree *object.Tree
    87→	if headCommit.NumParents() > 0 {
    88→		if parent, err := headCommit.Parent(0); err == nil {
    89→			if pTree, err := parent.Tree(); err == nil {
    90→				parentTree = pTree
    91→			}
    92→		}
    93→	}
    94→
    95→	// Check each file in filesTouched
    96→	for _, filePath := range filesTouched {
    97→		// Get file from HEAD tree (the committed content)
    98→		headFile, err := headTree.File(filePath)
    99→		if err != nil {
   100→			// File not in HEAD commit. This happens when:
   101→			// - The session created/modified the file but user deleted it before committing
   102→			// - The file was staged as a deletion (git rm)
   103→			// In both cases, the session's work on this file is not in the commit,
   104→			// so it doesn't contribute to overlap. Continue checking other files.
   105→			continue
   106→		}
   107→
   108→		// Check if this is a modified file (exists in parent) or new file
   109→		isModified := false
   110→		if parentTree != nil {
   111→			if _, err := parentTree.File(filePath); err == nil {
   112→				isModified = true
   113→			}
   114→		}
   115→
   116→		// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)
   117→		if isModified {
   118→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap",
   119→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   120→			)
   121→			return true
   122→		}
   123→
   124→		// For new files, check content against shadow branch
   125→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(filePath)
   126→		if err != nil {
   127→			// File not in shadow branch - this shouldn't happen but skip it
   128→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: file in filesTouched but not in shadow branch",
   129→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   130→			)
   131→			continue
   132→		}
   133→
   134→		// Compare by hash (blob hash) - exact content match required for new files
   135→		if headFile.Hash == shadowFile.Hash {
   136→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content match found",
   137→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   138→				slog.String("hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   139→			)
   140→			return true
   141→		}
   142→
   143→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content mismatch (may be reverted & replaced)",
   144→			slog.String("file", filePath),
   145→			slog.String("head_hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   146→			slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()),
   147→		)
   148→	}
   149→
   150→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: no overlapping files found",
   151→		slog.Int("files_checked", len(filesTouched)),
   152→	)
   153→	return false
   154→}
   155→
   156→// stagedFilesOverlapWithContent checks if any staged file overlaps with filesTouched,
   157→// distinguishing between modified files (always overlap) and new files (check content).
   158→//
   159→// For modified files (already exist in HEAD), we count as overlap because the user
   160→// is editing the session's work. For new files (don't exist in HEAD), we require
   161→// content match to detect the "reverted and replaced" scenario.
   162→//
   163→// This is used in PrepareCommitMsg for carry-forward scenarios.
   164→func stagedFilesOverlapWithContent(repo *git.Repository, shadowTree *object.Tree, stagedFiles, filesTouched []string) bool {
   165→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   166→
   167→	// Build set of filesTouched for quick lookup
   168→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
   169→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   170→		touchedSet[f] = true
   171→	}
   172→
   173→	// Get HEAD tree to determine if files are being modified or newly created
   174→	head, err := repo.Head()
   175→	if err != nil {
   176→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD, falling back to filename check",
   177→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   178→		)
   179→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   180→	}
   181→	headCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   182→	if err != nil {
   183→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD commit, falling back to filename check",
   184→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   185→		)
   186→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   187→	}
   188→	headTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
   189→	if err != nil {
   190→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD tree, falling back to filename check",
   191→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   192→		)
   193→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   194→	}
   195→
   196→	// Get the git index to access staged file hashes
   197→	idx, err := repo.Storer.Index()
   198→	if err != nil {
   199→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get index, falling back to filename check",
   200→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   201→		)
   202→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   203→	}
   204→
   205→	// Build a map of index entries for O(1) lookup (avoid O(n*m) nested loop)
   206→	indexEntries := make(map[string]plumbing.Hash, len(idx.Entries))
   207→	for _, entry := range idx.Entries {
   208→		indexEntries[entry.Name] = entry.Hash
   209→	}
   210→
   211→	// Check each staged file
   212→	for _, stagedPath := range stagedFiles {
   213→		if !touchedSet[stagedPath] {
   214→			continue // Not in filesTouched, skip
   215→		}
   216→
   217→		// Check if this is a modified file (exists in HEAD) or new file
   218→		_, headErr := headTree.File(stagedPath)
   219→		isModified := headErr == nil
   220→
   221→		// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)
   222→		if isModified {
   223→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap",
   224→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   225→			)
   226→			return true
   227→		}
   228→
   229→		// For new files, check content against shadow branch
   230→		stagedHash, found := indexEntries[stagedPath]
   231→		if !found {
   232→			continue // Not in index (shouldn't happen but be safe)
   233→		}
   234→
   235→		// Get file from shadow branch tree
   236→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(stagedPath)
   237→		if err != nil {
   238→			// File not in shadow branch - doesn't count as content match
   239→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: file not in shadow tree",
   240→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   241→			)
   242→			continue
   243→		}
   244→
   245→		// Compare hashes - for new files, require exact content match
   246→		if stagedHash == shadowFile.Hash {
   247→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: new file content match found",
   248→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   249→				slog.String("hash", stagedHash.String()),
   250→			)
   251→			return true
   252→		}
   253→
   254→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: new file content mismatch (may be reverted & replaced)",
   255→			slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   256→			slog.String("staged_hash", stagedHash.String()),
   257→			slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()),
   258→		)
   259→	}
   260→
   261→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: no overlapping files found",
   262→		slog.Int("staged_files", len(stagedFiles)),
   263→		slog.Int("files_touched", len(filesTouched)),
   264→	)
   265→	return false
   266→}
   267→
   268→// hasOverlappingFiles checks if any file in stagedFiles appears in filesTouched.
   269→// This is a fallback when content-aware comparison isn't possible.
   270→func hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched []string) bool {
   271→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
   272→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   273→		touchedSet[f] = true
   274→	}
   275→
   276→	for _, staged := range stagedFiles {
   277→		if touchedSet[staged] {
   278→			return true
   279→		}
   280→	}
   281→	return false
   282→}
   283→
   284→// filesWithRemainingAgentChanges returns files from filesTouched that still have
   285→// uncommitted agent changes. This is used for carry-forward after partial commits.
   286→//
   287→// A file has remaining agent changes if:
   288→//   - It wasn't committed at all (not in committedFiles), OR
   289→//   - It was committed but the committed content doesn't match the shadow branch
   290→//     (user committed partial changes, e.g., via git add -p)
   291→//
   292→// Falls back to file-level subtraction if shadow branch is unavailable.
   293→func filesWithRemainingAgentChanges(
   294→	repo *git.Repository,
   295→	shadowBranchName string,
   296→	headCommit *object.Commit,
   297→	filesTouched []string,
   298→	committedFiles map[string]struct{},
   299→) []string {
   300→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   301→
   302→	// Get HEAD commit tree (the committed content)
   303→	commitTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
   304→	if err != nil {
   305→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get commit tree, falling back to file subtraction",
   306→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   307→		)
   308→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   309→	}
   310→
   311→	// Get shadow branch tree (the session's full content)
   312→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   313→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   314→	if err != nil {
   315→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: shadow branch not found, falling back to file subtraction",
   316→			slog.String("branch", shadowBranchName),
   317→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   318→		)
   319→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   320→	}
   321→
   322→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
   323→	if err != nil {
   324→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get shadow commit, falling back to file subtraction",
   325→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   326→		)
   327→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   328→	}
   329→
   330→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
   331→	if err != nil {
   332→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get shadow tree, falling back to file subtraction",
   333→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   334→		)
   335→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   336→	}
   337→
   338→	var remaining []string
   339→
   340→	for _, filePath := range filesTouched {
   341→		// If file wasn't committed at all, it definitely has remaining changes
   342→		if _, wasCommitted := committedFiles[filePath]; !wasCommitted {
   343→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   344→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not committed, keeping",
   345→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   346→			)
   347→			continue
   348→		}
   349→
   350→		// File was committed - check if committed content matches shadow branch
   351→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(filePath)
   352→		if err != nil {
   353→			// File not in shadow branch - nothing to carry forward for this file
   354→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not in shadow branch, skipping",
   355→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   356→			)
   357→			continue
   358→		}
   359→
   360→		commitFile, err := commitTree.File(filePath)
   361→		if err != nil {
   362→			// File not in commit tree (deleted?) - keep it if it's in shadow
   363→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   364→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not in commit tree but in shadow, keeping",
   365→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   366→			)
   367→			continue
   368→		}
   369→
   370→		// Compare hashes - if different, there are still uncommitted agent changes
   371→		if commitFile.Hash != shadowFile.Hash {
   372→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   373→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: content mismatch, keeping for carry-forward",
   374→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   375→				slog.String("commit_hash", commitFile.Hash.String()[:7]),
   376→				slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()[:7]),
   377→			)
   378→		} else {
   379→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: content fully committed",
   380→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   381→			)
   382→		}
   383→	}
   384→
   385→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: result",
   386→		slog.Int("files_touched", len(filesTouched)),
   387→		slog.Int("committed_files", len(committedFiles)),
   388→		slog.Int("remaining_files", len(remaining)),
   389→	)
   390→
   391→	return remaining
   392→}
   393→
   394→// subtractFilesByName returns files from filesTouched that are NOT in committedFiles.
   395→// This is a fallback when content-aware comparison isn't possible.
   396→func subtractFilesByName(filesTouched []string, committedFiles map[string]struct{}) []string {
   397→	var remaining []string
   398→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   399→		if _, committed := committedFiles[f]; !committed {
   400→			remaining = append(remaining, f)
   401→		}
   402→	}
   403→	return remaining
   404→}
   405→

     3→package session
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"strings"
     8→	"time"
     9→)
    10→
    11→// Phase represents the lifecycle stage of a session.
    12→type Phase string
    13→
    14→const (
    15→	PhaseActive Phase = "active"
    16→	PhaseIdle   Phase = "idle"
    17→	PhaseEnded  Phase = "ended"
    18→)
    19→
    20→// allPhases is the canonical list of phases for enumeration (e.g., diagram generation).
    21→var allPhases = []Phase{PhaseIdle, PhaseActive, PhaseEnded}
    22→
    23→// PhaseFromString normalizes a phase string, treating empty or unknown values
    24→// as PhaseIdle for backward compatibility with pre-state-machine session files.
    25→func PhaseFromString(s string) Phase {
    26→	switch Phase(s) {
    27→	case PhaseActive, "active_committed":
    28→		// "active_committed" was removed with the 1:1 checkpoint model.
    29→		// It previously meant "agent active + commit happened during turn".
    30→		// Normalize to ACTIVE so HandleTurnEnd can finalize any pending checkpoints.
    31→		return PhaseActive
    32→	case PhaseIdle:
    33→		return PhaseIdle
    34→	case PhaseEnded:
    35→		return PhaseEnded
    36→	default:
    37→		// Backward compat: truly unknown phases normalize to idle.
    38→		return PhaseIdle
    39→	}
    40→}
    41→
    42→// IsActive reports whether the phase represents an active agent turn.
    43→func (p Phase) IsActive() bool {
    44→	return p == PhaseActive
    45→}
    46→
    47→// Event represents something that happened to a session.
    48→type Event int
    49→
    50→const (
    51→	EventTurnStart    Event = iota // Agent begins working on a prompt
    52→	EventTurnEnd                   // Agent finishes its turn
    53→	EventGitCommit                 // A git commit was made (PostCommit hook)
    54→	EventSessionStart              // Session process started (SessionStart hook)
    55→	EventSessionStop               // Session process ended (SessionStop hook)
    56→)
    57→
    58→// allEvents is the canonical list of events for enumeration.
    59→var allEvents = []Event{EventTurnStart, EventTurnEnd, EventGitCommit, EventSessionStart, EventSessionStop}
    60→
    61→// String returns a human-readable name for the event.
    62→func (e Event) String() string {
    63→	switch e {
    64→	case EventTurnStart:
    65→		return "TurnStart"
    66→	case EventTurnEnd:
    67→		return "TurnEnd"
    68→	case EventGitCommit:
    69→		return "GitCommit"
    70→	case EventSessionStart:
    71→		return "SessionStart"
    72→	case EventSessionStop:
    73→		return "SessionStop"
    74→	default:
    75→		return fmt.Sprintf("Event(%d)", int(e))
    76→	}
    77→}
    78→
    79→// Action represents a side effect that should be performed after a transition.
    80→// The caller is responsible for executing these -- the state machine only declares them.
    81→type Action int
    82→
    83→const (
    84→	ActionCondense               Action = iota // Condense session data to permanent storage
    85→	ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched               // Condense only if FilesTouched is non-empty
    86→	ActionDiscardIfNoFiles                     // Discard session if FilesTouched is empty
    87→	ActionWarnStaleSession                     // Warn user about stale session(s)
    88→	ActionClearEndedAt                         // Clear EndedAt timestamp (session re-entering)
    89→	ActionUpdateLastInteraction                // Update LastInteractionTime
    90→)
    91→
    92→// String returns a human-readable name for the action.
    93→func (a Action) String() string {
    94→	switch a {
    95→	case ActionCondense:
    96→		return "Condense"
    97→	case ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched:
    98→		return "CondenseIfFilesTouched"
    99→	case ActionDiscardIfNoFiles:
   100→		return "DiscardIfNoFiles"
   101→	case ActionWarnStaleSession:
   102→		return "WarnStaleSession"
   103→	case ActionClearEndedAt:
   104→		return "ClearEndedAt"
   105→	case ActionUpdateLastInteraction:
   106→		return "UpdateLastInteraction"
   107→	default:
   108→		return fmt.Sprintf("Action(%d)", int(a))
   109→	}
   110→}
   111→
   112→// TransitionContext provides read-only context for transitions that need
   113→// to inspect session state without mutating it.
   114→type TransitionContext struct {
   115→	HasFilesTouched    bool // len(FilesTouched) > 0
   116→	IsRebaseInProgress bool // .git/rebase-merge/ or .git/rebase-apply/ exists
   117→}
   118→
   119→// TransitionResult holds the outcome of a state machine transition.
   120→type TransitionResult struct {
   121→	NewPhase Phase
   122→	Actions  []Action
   123→}
   124→
   125→// Transition computes the next phase and required actions given the current
   126→// phase and an event. This is a pure function with no side effects.
   127→//
   128→// Empty or unknown phase values are normalized to PhaseIdle for backward
   129→// compatibility with session state files created before phase tracking.
   130→func Transition(current Phase, event Event, ctx TransitionContext) TransitionResult {
   131→	// Normalize empty/unknown phase to idle for backward compatibility.
   132→	current = PhaseFromString(string(current))
   133→
   134→	switch current {
   135→	case PhaseIdle:
   136→		return transitionFromIdle(event, ctx)
   137→	case PhaseActive:
   138→		return transitionFromActive(event, ctx)
   139→	case PhaseEnded:
   140→		return transitionFromEnded(event, ctx)
   141→	default:
   142→		// PhaseFromString guarantees we only get known phases, but the
   143→		// exhaustive linter requires handling the default case.
   144→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseIdle}
   145→	}
   146→}
   147→
   148→func transitionFromIdle(event Event, ctx TransitionContext) TransitionResult {
   149→	switch event {
   150→	case EventTurnStart:
   151→		return TransitionResult{
   152→			NewPhase: PhaseActive,
   153→			Actions:  []Action{ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   154→		}
   155→	case EventTurnEnd:
   156→		// Turn end while idle is a no-op (no active turn to end).
   157→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseIdle}
   158→	case EventGitCommit:
   159→		if ctx.IsRebaseInProgress {
   160→			return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseIdle}
   161→		}
   162→		return TransitionResult{
   163→			NewPhase: PhaseIdle,
   164→			Actions:  []Action{ActionCondense, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   165→		}
   166→	case EventSessionStart:
   167→		// Already condensable, no-op.
   168→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseIdle}
   169→	case EventSessionStop:
   170→		return TransitionResult{
   171→			NewPhase: PhaseEnded,
   172→			Actions:  []Action{ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   173→		}
   174→	default:
   175→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseIdle}
   176→	}
   177→}
   178→
   179→func transitionFromActive(event Event, ctx TransitionContext) TransitionResult {
   180→	switch event {
   181→	case EventTurnStart:
   182→		// Ctrl-C recovery: agent crashed or user interrupted mid-turn.
   183→		return TransitionResult{
   184→			NewPhase: PhaseActive,
   185→			Actions:  []Action{ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   186→		}
   187→	case EventTurnEnd:
   188→		return TransitionResult{
   189→			NewPhase: PhaseIdle,
   190→			Actions:  []Action{ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   191→		}
   192→	case EventGitCommit:
   193→		if ctx.IsRebaseInProgress {
   194→			return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseActive}
   195→		}
   196→		return TransitionResult{
   197→			NewPhase: PhaseActive,
   198→			Actions:  []Action{ActionCondense, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   199→		}
   200→	case EventSessionStart:
   201→		return TransitionResult{
   202→			NewPhase: PhaseActive,
   203→			Actions:  []Action{ActionWarnStaleSession},
   204→		}
   205→	case EventSessionStop:
   206→		return TransitionResult{
   207→			NewPhase: PhaseEnded,
   208→			Actions:  []Action{ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   209→		}
   210→	default:
   211→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseActive}
   212→	}
   213→}
   214→
   215→func transitionFromEnded(event Event, ctx TransitionContext) TransitionResult {
   216→	switch event {
   217→	case EventTurnStart:
   218→		return TransitionResult{
   219→			NewPhase: PhaseActive,
   220→			Actions:  []Action{ActionClearEndedAt, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   221→		}
   222→	case EventTurnEnd:
   223→		// Turn end while ended is a no-op.
   224→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseEnded}
   225→	case EventGitCommit:
   226→		if ctx.IsRebaseInProgress {
   227→			return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseEnded}
   228→		}
   229→		if ctx.HasFilesTouched {
   230→			return TransitionResult{
   231→				NewPhase: PhaseEnded,
   232→				Actions:  []Action{ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   233→			}
   234→		}
   235→		return TransitionResult{
   236→			NewPhase: PhaseEnded,
   237→			Actions:  []Action{ActionDiscardIfNoFiles, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   238→		}
   239→	case EventSessionStart:
   240→		return TransitionResult{
   241→			NewPhase: PhaseIdle,
   242→			Actions:  []Action{ActionClearEndedAt},
   243→		}
   244→	case EventSessionStop:
   245→		// Already ended, no-op.
   246→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseEnded}
   247→	default:
   248→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseEnded}
   249→	}
   250→}
   251→
   252→// ApplyCommonActions applies the common (non-strategy-specific) actions from a
   253→// TransitionResult to the given State. It updates Phase, LastInteractionTime,
   254→// and EndedAt as indicated by the transition.
   255→//
   256→// Returns the subset of actions that require strategy-specific handling
   257→// (e.g., ActionCondense, ActionWarnStaleSession).
   258→// The caller is responsible for dispatching those.
   259→func ApplyCommonActions(state *State, result TransitionResult) []Action {
   260→	state.Phase = result.NewPhase
   261→
   262→	var remaining []Action
   263→	for _, action := range result.Actions {
   264→		switch action {
   265→		case ActionUpdateLastInteraction:
   266→			now := time.Now()
   267→			state.LastInteractionTime = &now
   268→		case ActionClearEndedAt:
   269→			state.EndedAt = nil
   270→		case ActionCondense, ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched, ActionDiscardIfNoFiles,
   271→			ActionWarnStaleSession:
   272→			// Strategy-specific actions — pass through to caller.
   273→			remaining = append(remaining, action)
   274→		}
   275→	}
   276→	return remaining
   277→}
   278→
   279→// MermaidDiagram generates a Mermaid state diagram from the transition table.
   280→// The diagram is derived by calling Transition() for representative context
   281→// variants per phase/event, so it stays in sync with the implementation.
   282→func MermaidDiagram() string {
   283→	var b strings.Builder
   284→	b.WriteString("stateDiagram-v2\n")
   285→
   286→	// State declarations with descriptions.
   287→	b.WriteString("    state \"IDLE\" as idle\n")
   288→	b.WriteString("    state \"ACTIVE\" as active\n")
   289→	b.WriteString("    state \"ENDED\" as ended\n")
   290→	b.WriteString("\n")
   291→
   292→	// Context variants for GitCommit: rebase, files/no-files.
   293→	type contextVariant struct {
   294→		label string
   295→		ctx   TransitionContext
   296→	}
   297→
   298→	// For each phase/event, generate edges.
   299→	for _, phase := range allPhases {
   300→		for _, event := range allEvents {
   301→			var variants []contextVariant
   302→			switch {
   303→			case event == EventGitCommit && phase == PhaseEnded:
   304→				// HasFilesTouched only affects PhaseEnded transitions;
   305→				// other phases produce identical edges for both values.
   306→				variants = []contextVariant{
   307→					{"[files]", TransitionContext{HasFilesTouched: true}},
   308→					{"[no files]", TransitionContext{HasFilesTouched: false}},
   309→					{"[rebase]", TransitionContext{IsRebaseInProgress: true}},
   310→				}
   311→			case event == EventGitCommit:
   312→				variants = []contextVariant{
   313→					{"", TransitionContext{}},
   314→					{"[rebase]", TransitionContext{IsRebaseInProgress: true}},
   315→				}
   316→			default:
   317→				variants = []contextVariant{
   318→					{"", TransitionContext{}},
   319→				}
   320→			}
   321→
   322→			for _, v := range variants {
   323→				result := Transition(phase, event, v.ctx)
   324→
   325→				// Build edge label.
   326→				label := event.String()
   327→				if v.label != "" {
   328→					label += " " + v.label
   329→				}
   330→				if len(result.Actions) > 0 {
   331→					actionNames := make([]string, 0, len(result.Actions))
   332→					for _, a := range result.Actions {
   333→						actionNames = append(actionNames, a.String())
   334→					}
   335→					label += " / " + strings.Join(actionNames, ", ")
   336→				}
   337→
   338→				from := string(phase)
   339→				to := string(result.NewPhase)
   340→
   341→				fmt.Fprintf(&b, "    %s --> %s : %s\n", from, to, label)
   342→			}
   343→		}
   344→	}
   345→
   346→	return b.String()
   347→}
   348→
```

Now I have a thorough understanding of the PR. Here's my consolidated review:

---

## Code Review: PR #325 — Every checkpoint should have only one commit

### Overview

This PR changes the checkpoint model from **1:N** (one checkpoint ID spanning multiple commits) to **1:1** (every commit gets a unique checkpoint ID). It introduces a **provisional-then-finalize** pattern: mid-turn commits get a provisional transcript that's replaced with the full transcript at turn end. The `ACTIVE_COMMITTED` phase is removed, simplifying the state machine to 3 phases.

**Verdict: Approve with minor suggestions**

---

### Strengths

- **Clean simplification.** Removing `ACTIVE_COMMITTED` reduces the state machine from 4 phases to 3. The transition table in `phase.go` is easy to follow — `ACTIVE + GitCommit → ACTIVE` with `ActionCondense` is elegant.

- **Robust degradation.** `finalizeAllTurnCheckpoints` (`manual_commit_hooks.go:1598`) is best-effort: it logs warnings and clears `TurnCheckpointIDs` even on failure, so partial finalization never blocks future turns. Provisional data always persists.

- **Content-aware overlap detection** (`content_overlap.go`). The two-tier design (modified files always count, new files require content match) correctly handles the "revert and replace" scenario. The fail-open fallbacks when git state is unreadable are the right call.

- **Backward compatibility.** `PhaseFromString` (`phase.go:27`) normalizes `"active_committed"` to `PhaseActive` so existing session state files work after upgrade, and pending finalization isn't lost.

- **Comprehensive test coverage.** 8+ integration test scenarios, dedicated unit tests for `TurnCheckpointIDs` lifecycle, content overlap, carry-forward, and multi-commit-per-turn finalization.

---

### Issues & Suggestions

#### 1. Double redaction in `UpdateCommitted` (minor perf)

`finalizeAllTurnCheckpoints` (`manual_commit_hooks.go:1638`) redacts the transcript before passing to `UpdateCommitted`, which redacts again at `committed.go:1079`. Harmless but wastes CPU on large transcripts.

**Suggestion:** Either skip redaction in `finalizeAllTurnCheckpoints` (document that `UpdateCommitted` handles it), or add a `skipRedaction` option to `UpdateCommitted`. Low priority since correctness isn't affected.

#### 2. Subtle coupling in `TurnCheckpointIDs` guard (`manual_commit_hooks.go:643`)

```go
if condensed && state.Phase.IsActive() {
    state.TurnCheckpointIDs = append(state.TurnCheckpointIDs, checkpointID.String())
}
```

This runs **after** `TransitionAndLog` has already updated the phase. It works because `ACTIVE + GitCommit → ACTIVE` in the state machine, but if that transition ever changed (e.g., to IDLE), the guard would silently stop recording checkpoint IDs without any logging. Consider adding a comment or an assertion documenting this coupling.

#### 3. Carry-forward resets `StepCount`/`LastCheckpointID` but preserves `TurnCheckpointIDs` (clarity)

At `manual_commit_hooks.go:~662`, after carry-forward, the code resets some state fields but intentionally keeps `TurnCheckpointIDs` because those checkpoints still need finalization at turn end. This is correct but non-obvious — a comment explaining this would prevent accidental "cleanup" in future.

#### 4. `filesOverlapWithContent` skips deleted files silently (`content_overlap.go:99-106`)

When a session-touched file is staged as a deletion (`git rm`), it's skipped (no overlap). This is the right default, but consider: if the session created a file and the user immediately `git rm`s it, the session's work was present then removed — should this count as overlap for checkpoint purposes? Currently it doesn't, which means no checkpoint trailer on a commit that undoes session work. This may be intentional but isn't documented.

#### 5. Missing test: `HandleTurnEnd` partial failure

The integration tests cover the happy path (all checkpoints finalized) and the fully-failed path (no transcript). There's no test for **partial failure** — e.g., 3 `TurnCheckpointIDs` where the 2nd `UpdateCommitted` call fails. The code handles this correctly (continues processing, returns error count), but a test would lock that behavior.

#### 6. `TurnEndHandler` interface change is a breaking API change

`HandleTurnEnd` no longer takes `[]session.Action` (`strategy.go:469`). The auto-commit strategy at `auto_commit.go:946` clears `TurnCheckpointIDs` on prompt start. Verify that no external implementations of this interface exist (community forks, plugins). If the interface is internal-only, this is fine.

---

### Architecture Assessment

The provisional-then-finalize approach is sound. Key invariants:

1. Every commit gets a checkpoint ID → enforced in PostCommit
2. Provisional data is always written before returning → crash-safe
3. Finalization is best-effort → no data loss on failure, just partial transcripts
4. `TurnCheckpointIDs` is cleared on every exit path → no unbounded growth

The carry-forward logic (`manual_commit_hooks.go:647-663`) correctly splits uncommitted work onto a new shadow branch so subsequent commits get their own checkpoint IDs. The content-aware comparison in `filesWithRemainingAgentChanges` properly handles `git add -p` partial staging.

### Test Quality

- All tests use `t.Parallel()` appropriately (omitted only for `t.Chdir` tests)
- Integration tests cover: basic finalization, carry-forward, multi-commit-per-turn, content overlap detection, reverted files, session reset, and depleted sessions
- The `content_overlap_test.go` has 12 unit tests covering all branches
- Low flakiness risk — tests use `t.TempDir()` with no shared state

### Summary

This is a well-executed simplification. The 1:1 model is easier to reason about than 1:N, and the provisional-then-finalize pattern handles the timing gap cleanly. The main suggestions are around documentation of subtle invariants (points 2, 3) and a missing partial-failure test (point 5). No blocking issues.

  1012→// UpdateCommitted replaces the transcript, prompts, and context for an existing
  1013→// committed checkpoint. Uses replace semantics: the full session transcript is
  1014→// written, replacing whatever was stored at initial condensation time.
  1015→//
  1016→// This is called at stop time to finalize all checkpoints from the current turn
  1017→// with the complete session transcript (from prompt to stop event).
  1018→//
  1019→// Returns ErrCheckpointNotFound if the checkpoint doesn't exist.
  1020→func (s *GitStore) UpdateCommitted(ctx context.Context, opts UpdateCommittedOptions) error {
  1021→	if opts.CheckpointID.IsEmpty() {
  1022→		return errors.New("invalid update options: checkpoint ID is required")
  1023→	}
  1024→
  1025→	// Ensure sessions branch exists
  1026→	if err := s.ensureSessionsBranch(); err != nil {
  1027→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to ensure sessions branch: %w", err)
  1028→	}
  1029→
  1030→	// Get current branch tip and flatten tree
  1031→	ref, entries, err := s.getSessionsBranchEntries()
  1032→	if err != nil {
  1033→		return err
  1034→	}
  1035→
  1036→	// Read root CheckpointSummary to find the session slot
  1037→	basePath := opts.CheckpointID.Path() + "/"
  1038→	rootMetadataPath := basePath + paths.MetadataFileName
  1039→	entry, exists := entries[rootMetadataPath]
  1040→	if !exists {
  1041→		return ErrCheckpointNotFound
  1042→	}
  1043→
  1044→	checkpointSummary, err := s.readSummaryFromBlob(entry.Hash)
  1045→	if err != nil {
  1046→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to read checkpoint summary: %w", err)
  1047→	}
  1048→	if len(checkpointSummary.Sessions) == 0 {
  1049→		return ErrCheckpointNotFound
  1050→	}
  1051→
  1052→	// Find session index matching opts.SessionID
  1053→	sessionIndex := -1
  1054→	for i := range len(checkpointSummary.Sessions) {
  1055→		metaPath := fmt.Sprintf("%s%d/%s", basePath, i, paths.MetadataFileName)
  1056→		if metaEntry, metaExists := entries[metaPath]; metaExists {
  1057→			meta, metaErr := s.readMetadataFromBlob(metaEntry.Hash)
  1058→			if metaErr == nil && meta.SessionID == opts.SessionID {
  1059→				sessionIndex = i
  1060→				break
  1061→			}
  1062→		}
  1063→	}
  1064→	if sessionIndex == -1 {
  1065→		// Fall back to latest session; log so mismatches are diagnosable.
  1066→		sessionIndex = len(checkpointSummary.Sessions) - 1
  1067→		logging.Debug(ctx, "UpdateCommitted: session ID not found, falling back to latest",
  1068→			slog.String("session_id", opts.SessionID),
  1069→			slog.String("checkpoint_id", string(opts.CheckpointID)),
  1070→			slog.Int("fallback_index", sessionIndex),
  1071→		)
  1072→	}
  1073→
  1074→	sessionPath := fmt.Sprintf("%s%d/", basePath, sessionIndex)
  1075→
  1076→	// Replace transcript (full replace, not append)
  1077→	// Apply redaction as safety net (caller should redact, but we ensure it here)
  1078→	if len(opts.Transcript) > 0 {
  1079→		transcript, err := redact.JSONLBytes(opts.Transcript)
  1080→		if err != nil {
  1081→			return fmt.Errorf("failed to redact transcript secrets: %w", err)
  1082→		}
  1083→		if err := s.replaceTranscript(transcript, opts.Agent, sessionPath, entries); err != nil {
  1084→			return fmt.Errorf("failed to replace transcript: %w", err)
  1085→		}
  1086→	}
  1087→
  1088→	// Replace prompts (apply redaction as safety net)
  1089→	if len(opts.Prompts) > 0 {
  1090→		promptContent := redact.String(strings.Join(opts.Prompts, "\n\n---\n\n"))
  1091→		blobHash, err := CreateBlobFromContent(s.repo, []byte(promptContent))
  1092→		if err != nil {
  1093→			return fmt.Errorf("failed to create prompt blob: %w", err)
  1094→		}
  1095→		entries[sessionPath+paths.PromptFileName] = object.TreeEntry{
  1096→			Name: sessionPath + paths.PromptFileName,
  1097→			Mode: filemode.Regular,
  1098→			Hash: blobHash,
  1099→		}

> AGENT

Now I see the issue clearly. `WriteCommitted` takes `AuthorName`/`AuthorEmail` from the options (line 98), but `UpdateCommitted` (the finalization commit) falls back to `getGitAuthorFromRepo` (line 1121) — it has no author fields in `UpdateCommittedOptions` at all.

Let me check what the callers pass for `WriteCommitted` to understand what author info the initial commit gets vs the finalization.

   106→// CondenseSession condenses a session's shadow branch to permanent storage.
   107→// checkpointID is the 12-hex-char value from the Entire-Checkpoint trailer.
   108→// Metadata is stored at sharded path: <checkpoint_id[:2]>/<checkpoint_id[2:]>/
   109→// Uses checkpoint.GitStore.WriteCommitted for the git operations.
   110→//
   111→// For mid-session commits (no Stop/SaveChanges called yet), the shadow branch may not exist.
   112→// In this case, data is extracted from the live transcript instead.
   113→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) CondenseSession(repo *git.Repository, checkpointID id.CheckpointID, state *SessionState) (*CondenseResult, error) {
   114→	// Get shadow branch (may not exist for mid-session commits)
   115→	shadowBranchName := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit, state.WorktreeID)
   116→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   117→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   118→	hasShadowBranch := err == nil
   119→
   120→	var sessionData *ExtractedSessionData
   121→	if hasShadowBranch {
   122→		// Extract session data from the shadow branch (with live transcript fallback).
   123→		// Use tracked files from session state instead of collecting all files from tree.
   124→		// Pass agent type to handle different transcript formats (JSONL for Claude, JSON for Gemini).
   125→		// Pass live transcript path so condensation reads the current file rather than a
   126→		// potentially stale shadow branch copy (SaveChanges may have been skipped if the
   127→		// last turn had no code changes).
   128→		// Pass CheckpointTranscriptStart for accurate token calculation (line offset for Claude, message index for Gemini).
   129→		sessionData, err = s.extractSessionData(repo, ref.Hash(), state.SessionID, state.FilesTouched, state.AgentType, state.TranscriptPath, state.CheckpointTranscriptStart)
   130→		if err != nil {
   131→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to extract session data: %w", err)
   132→		}
   133→	} else {
   134→		// No shadow branch: mid-session commit before Stop/SaveChanges.
   135→		// Extract data directly from live transcript.
   136→		if state.TranscriptPath == "" {
   137→			return nil, errors.New("shadow branch not found and no live transcript available")
   138→		}
   139→		sessionData, err = s.extractSessionDataFromLiveTranscript(state)
   140→		if err != nil {
   141→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to extract session data from live transcript: %w", err)
   142→		}
   143→	}
   144→
   145→	// Get checkpoint store
   146→	store, err := s.getCheckpointStore()
   147→	if err != nil {
   148→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get checkpoint store: %w", err)
   149→	}
   150→
   151→	// Get author info
   152→	authorName, authorEmail := GetGitAuthorFromRepo(repo)
   153→	// Attribution calculation requires shadow branch reference; skip if mid-session commit
   154→	var attribution *cpkg.InitialAttribution
   155→	if hasShadowBranch {
   156→		attribution = calculateSessionAttributions(repo, ref, sessionData, state)
   157→	}
   158→	// Get current branch name
   159→	branchName := GetCurrentBranchName(repo)
   160→
   161→	// Generate summary if enabled
   162→	var summary *cpkg.Summary
   163→	if settings.IsSummarizeEnabled() && len(sessionData.Transcript) > 0 {
   164→		logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "attribution")
   165→		summarizeCtx := logging.WithComponent(logCtx, "summarize")
   166→
   167→		// Scope transcript to this checkpoint's portion.
   168→		// For Claude Code (JSONL), CheckpointTranscriptStart is a line offset.
   169→		// For Gemini (JSON), CheckpointTranscriptStart is a message index.

The public `GetGitAuthorFromRepo` in `strategy/common.go:1457` also checks **global git config** as a fallback (`config.LoadConfig(config.GlobalScope)`), while the private `getGitAuthorFromRepo` in `checkpoint/committed.go:1362` only reads the repo config. These are different implementations that can return different results.

> AGENT

Clear. The "Finalize transcript" commit shows `Author: Unknown <unknown@local>` because `getGitAuthorFromRepo` in `checkpoint/committed.go:1362` only reads the local repo config. When git user is configured globally (not in `.git/config`), it doesn't find it. The initial `WriteCommitted` works because it gets author from `strategy/common.go:GetGitAuthorFromRepo` which checks both local and global config.

The fix is to update the private `getGitAuthorFromRepo` in `committed.go` to also check global config, matching the public version.

> AGENT

I've completed the root cause investigation. Here's the summary:

**Root cause:** `getGitAuthorFromRepo` in `checkpoint/committed.go:1362` only reads local repo config via `repo.Config()`. When git user is configured globally (`~/.gitconfig`) — which is the common setup — it falls back to `Unknown <unknown@local>`. The public `GetGitAuthorFromRepo` in `strategy/common.go:1457` correctly checks both local and global config.

**Affected call sites in committed.go:**
- Line 1121: `UpdateCommitted` ("Finalize transcript" commits) — **the reported bug**
- Line 996: `UpdateSummary` 
- Line 1198: `ensureSessionsBranch` ("Initialize metadata branch")

The fix is to add the global config fallback to the private `getGitAuthorFromRepo`. Let me write a failing test first, then fix.

  1362→// getGitAuthorFromRepo retrieves the git user.name and user.email from the repository config.
  1363→func getGitAuthorFromRepo(repo *git.Repository) (name, email string) {
  1364→	// Get repository config (includes local settings)
  1365→	cfg, err := repo.Config()
  1366→	if err == nil {
  1367→		name = cfg.User.Name
  1368→		email = cfg.User.Email
  1369→	}
  1370→
  1371→	// Provide sensible defaults if git user is not configured
  1372→	if name == "" {
  1373→		name = "Unknown"
  1374→	}
  1375→	if email == "" {
  1376→		email = "unknown@local"
  1377→	}
  1378→
  1379→	return name, email
  1380→}

  "old_string": "// getGitAuthorFromRepo retrieves the git user.name and user.email from the repository config.\nfunc getGitAuthorFromRepo(repo *git.Repository) (name, email string) {\n\t// Get repository config (includes local settings)\n\tcfg, err := repo.Config()\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\tname = cfg.User.Name\n\t\temail = cfg.User.Email\n\t}\n\n\t// Provide sensible defaults if git user is not configured\n\tif name == \"\" {\n\t\tname = \"Unknown\"\n\t}\n\tif email == \"\" {\n\t\temail = \"unknown@local\"\n\t}\n\n\treturn name, email\n}",
  "new_string": "// GetGitAuthorFromRepo retrieves the git user.name and user.email,\n// checking both the repository-local config and the global ~/.gitconfig.\nfunc GetGitAuthorFromRepo(repo *git.Repository) (name, email string) {\n\t// Get repository config (includes local settings)\n\tcfg, err := repo.Config()\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\tname = cfg.User.Name\n\t\temail = cfg.User.Email\n\t}\n\n\t// If not found in local config, try global config\n\tif name == \"\" || email == \"\" {\n\t\tglobalCfg, err := config.LoadConfig(config.GlobalScope)\n\t\tif err == nil {\n\t\t\tif name == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\tname = globalCfg.User.Name\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif email == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\temail = globalCfg.User.Email\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Provide sensible defaults if git user is not configured\n\tif name == \"\" {\n\t\tname = \"Unknown\"\n\t}\n\tif email == \"\" {\n\t\temail = \"unknown@local\"\n\t}\n\n\treturn name, email\n}"
}

  "old_string": "// GetGitAuthorFromRepo retrieves the git user.name and user.email from the repository config.\n// It checks local config first, then falls back to global config.\n// Returns (\"Unknown\", \"unknown@local\") if no user is configured - this allows\n// operations to proceed even without git user config, which is especially useful\n// for internal metadata commits on branches like entire/checkpoints/v1.\nfunc GetGitAuthorFromRepo(repo *git.Repository) (name, email string) {\n\t// Get repository config (includes local settings)\n\tcfg, err := repo.Config()\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\tname = cfg.User.Name\n\t\temail = cfg.User.Email\n\t}\n\n\t// If not found in local config, try global config\n\tif name == \"\" || email == \"\" {\n\t\tglobalCfg, err := config.LoadConfig(config.GlobalScope)\n\t\tif err == nil {\n\t\t\tif name == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\tname = globalCfg.User.Name\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif email == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\temail = globalCfg.User.Email\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Provide sensible defaults if git user is not configured\n\tif name == \"\" {\n\t\tname = \"Unknown\"\n\t}\n\tif email == \"\" {\n\t\temail = \"unknown@local\"\n\t}\n\n\treturn name, email\n}",
  "new_string": "// GetGitAuthorFromRepo retrieves the git user.name and user.email,\n// checking both the repository-local config and the global ~/.gitconfig.\n// Delegates to checkpoint.GetGitAuthorFromRepo — this wrapper exists so\n// callers within the strategy package don't need a qualified import.\nfunc GetGitAuthorFromRepo(repo *git.Repository) (name, email string) {\n\treturn checkpoint.GetGitAuthorFromRepo(repo)\n}"
}

> AGENT

All tests pass, including integration tests. Here's a summary of the changes:

## Changes Made

**Root cause:** `getGitAuthorFromRepo` in `checkpoint/committed.go` only read the repo-local `.git/config` for author info. When git user is configured globally (`~/.gitconfig`) — which is the common setup — it returned `Unknown <unknown@local>`. This affected all commits created by the checkpoint package: `UpdateCommitted` ("Finalize transcript"), `UpdateSummary`, and `ensureSessionsBranch`.

**Fix: Consolidate to a single `GetGitAuthorFromRepo`**

1. **`checkpoint/committed.go`** — Fixed and exported `GetGitAuthorFromRepo`: added global config fallback via `config.LoadConfig(config.GlobalScope)`, matching the behavior that `strategy/common.go` had.

2. **`strategy/common.go`** — Changed `GetGitAuthorFromRepo` to delegate to `checkpoint.GetGitAuthorFromRepo()`, eliminating the duplicate implementation. Removed now-unused `go-git/v5/config` import.

3. **`checkpoint/committed_update_test.go`** — Added 3 tests:
   - `TestUpdateCommitted_UsesCorrectAuthor` — end-to-end test verifying the "Finalize transcript" commit gets the correct author from global config
   - `TestGetGitAuthorFromRepo_GlobalFallback` — unit test for the global config path
   - `TestGetGitAuthorFromRepo_NoConfig` — unit test for the default fallback

+// TestUpdateCommitted_UsesCorrectAuthor verifies that the "Finalize transcript"
+// commit on entire/checkpoints/v1 gets the correct author from global git config,
+// not "Unknown <unknown@local>".
+func TestUpdateCommitted_UsesCorrectAuthor(t *testing.T) {
+	// Cannot use t.Parallel() because subtests use t.Setenv.
+
+	tests := []struct {
+		name        string
+		localName   string
+		localEmail  string
+		globalName  string
+		globalEmail string
+		wantName    string
+		wantEmail   string
+	}{
+		{
+			name:        "global config only",
+			globalName:  "Global User",
+			globalEmail: "global@example.com",
+			wantName:    "Global User",
+			wantEmail:   "global@example.com",
+		},
+		{
+			name:       "local config takes precedence",
+			localName:  "Local User",
+			localEmail: "local@example.com",
+			globalName: "Global User",
+			wantName:   "Local User",
+			wantEmail:  "local@example.com",
+		},
+	}
+
+	for _, tt := range tests {
+		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
+			// Isolate global git config by pointing HOME to a temp dir
+			home := t.TempDir()
+			t.Setenv("HOME", home)
+			t.Setenv("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", "")
+
+			// Write global .gitconfig if needed
+			if tt.globalName != "" || tt.globalEmail != "" {
+				globalCfg := "[user]\n"
+				if tt.globalName != "" {
+					globalCfg += "\tname = " + tt.globalName + "\n"
+				}
+				if tt.globalEmail != "" {
+					globalCfg += "\temail = " + tt.globalEmail + "\n"
+				}
+				if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(home, ".gitconfig"), []byte(globalCfg), 0o644); err != nil {
+					t.Fatalf("failed to write global gitconfig: %v", err)
+				}
+			}
+
+			// Create repo
+			dir := t.TempDir()
+			repo, err := git.PlainInit(dir, false)
+			if err != nil {
+				t.Fatalf("failed to init repo: %v", err)
+			}
+
+			// Set local config if needed
+			if tt.localName != "" || tt.localEmail != "" {
+				cfg, err := repo.Config()
+				if err != nil {
+					t.Fatalf("failed to get repo config: %v", err)
+				}
+				cfg.User.Name = tt.localName
+				cfg.User.Email = tt.localEmail
+				if err := repo.SetConfig(cfg); err != nil {
+					t.Fatalf("failed to set repo config: %v", err)
+				}
+			}
+
+			// Create initial commit so repo has HEAD
+			wt, err := repo.Worktree()
+			if err != nil {
+				t.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
+			}
+			readmeFile := filepath.Join(dir, "README.md")
+			if err := os.WriteFile(readmeFile, []byte("# Test"), 0o644); err != nil {
+				t.Fatalf("failed to write README: %v", err)
+			}
+			if _, err := wt.Add("README.md"); err != nil {
+				t.Fatalf("failed to add README: %v", err)
+			}
+			if _, err := wt.Commit("Initial commit", &git.CommitOptions{
+				Author: &object.Signature{Name: "Setup", Email: "setup@test.com"},
+			}); err != nil {
+				t.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
+			}
+
+			// Write initial checkpoint
+			store := NewGitStore(repo)
+			cpID := id.MustCheckpointID("a1b2c3d4e5f6")
+			err = store.WriteCommitted(context.Background(), WriteCommittedOptions{
+				CheckpointID: cpID,
+				SessionID:    "session-001",
+				Strategy:     "manual-commit",
+				Transcript:   []byte("provisional\n"),
+				AuthorName:   tt.wantName,
+				AuthorEmail:  tt.wantEmail,
+			})
+			if err != nil {
+				t.Fatalf("WriteCommitted() error = %v", err)
+			}
+
+			// Call UpdateCommitted — this is the operation under test
+			err = store.UpdateCommitted(context.Background(), UpdateCommittedOptions{
+				CheckpointID: cpID,
+				SessionID:    "session-001",
+				Transcript:   []byte("full transcript\n"),
+			})
+			if err != nil {
+				t.Fatalf("UpdateCommitted() error = %v", err)
+			}
+
+			// Read the latest commit on entire/checkpoints/v1 and verify author
+			ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName), true)
+			if err != nil {
+				t.Fatalf("failed to get sessions branch ref: %v", err)
+			}
+			commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
+			if err != nil {
+				t.Fatalf("failed to get commit: %v", err)
+			}
+
+			if commit.Author.Name != tt.wantName {
+				t.Errorf("commit author name = %q, want %q", commit.Author.Name, tt.wantName)
+			}
+			if commit.Author.Email != tt.wantEmail {
+				t.Errorf("commit author email = %q, want %q", commit.Author.Email, tt.wantEmail)
+			}
+		})
+	}
+}
+
+// TestGetGitAuthorFromRepo_GlobalFallback verifies that GetGitAuthorFromRepo
+// falls back to global git config when local config is empty.
+func TestGetGitAuthorFromRepo_GlobalFallback(t *testing.T) {
+	// Cannot use t.Parallel() because we use t.Setenv.
+
+	home := t.TempDir()
+	t.Setenv("HOME", home)
+	t.Setenv("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", "")
+
+	// Write global .gitconfig with user info
+	globalCfg := "[user]\n\tname = Global Author\n\temail = global@test.com\n"
+	if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(home, ".gitconfig"), []byte(globalCfg), 0o644); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("failed to write global gitconfig: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	// Create repo with NO local user config
+	dir := t.TempDir()
+	repo, err := git.PlainInit(dir, false)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("failed to init repo: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	name, email := GetGitAuthorFromRepo(repo)
+	if name != "Global Author" {
+		t.Errorf("name = %q, want %q", name, "Global Author")
+	}
+	if email != "global@test.com" {
+		t.Errorf("email = %q, want %q", email, "global@test.com")
+	}
+}
+
+// TestGetGitAuthorFromRepo_NoConfig verifies defaults when no config exists.
+func TestGetGitAuthorFromRepo_NoConfig(t *testing.T) {
+	// Cannot use t.Parallel() because we use t.Setenv.
+
+	home := t.TempDir() // Empty home — no .gitconfig
+	t.Setenv("HOME", home)
+	t.Setenv("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", "")
+
+	dir := t.TempDir()
+	repo, err := git.PlainInit(dir, false)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("failed to init repo: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	name, email := GetGitAuthorFromRepo(repo)
+	if name != "Unknown" {
+		t.Errorf("name = %q, want %q", name, "Unknown")
+	}
+	if email != "unknown@local" {
+		t.Errorf("email = %q, want %q", email, "unknown@local")
+	}
+}
+
+// Verify go-git config import is used (compile-time check).
+var _ = config.GlobalScope

> AGENT

## Code Review: PR #325 — Every checkpoint should have only one commit

### Overview

Changes the checkpoint model from **1:N** (one checkpoint ID spanning multiple commits) to **1:1** (every commit gets a unique checkpoint ID). Introduces a **provisional-then-finalize** pattern: mid-turn commits get a provisional transcript replaced with the full transcript at turn end. Removes `ACTIVE_COMMITTED` phase, simplifying the state machine to 3 phases.

**+7642 / -1600** across 46 files.

**Verdict: Approve with suggestions**

---

### Strengths

- **Clean simplification.** `ACTIVE + GitCommit → ACTIVE` with `ActionCondense` replaces the 4-phase model. The transition table in `phase.go` is easy to follow.
- **Robust degradation.** `finalizeAllTurnCheckpoints` (`manual_commit_hooks.go:1598`) is best-effort — logs warnings and clears `TurnCheckpointIDs` on every exit path. Provisional data is always persisted first, so failures degrade to partial transcripts rather than data loss.
- **Content-aware overlap detection** (`content_overlap.go`). Two-tier design: modified files always count as overlap, new files require content hash match. Correctly handles "revert and replace" scenario. Fail-open fallbacks are the right call.
- **Backward compatibility.** `PhaseFromString` normalizes `"active_committed"` → `PhaseActive` so existing session state files work after upgrade and pending finalization isn't lost.
- **Comprehensive tests.** 8+ integration scenarios, dedicated unit tests for `TurnCheckpointIDs` lifecycle, content overlap, carry-forward, multi-commit-per-turn, and session reset.

---

### Issues & Suggestions

#### 1. Subtle coupling in `TurnCheckpointIDs` guard (minor)

`manual_commit_hooks.go:643`:
```go
if condensed && state.Phase.IsActive() {
    state.TurnCheckpointIDs = append(...)
}
```

This runs **after** `TransitionAndLog` updates the phase. Works because `ACTIVE + GitCommit → ACTIVE`, but if that transition changed, the guard would silently stop recording IDs. Worth a comment documenting the invariant.

#### 2. Carry-forward preserves `TurnCheckpointIDs` intentionally (clarity)

After carry-forward (~`manual_commit_hooks.go:662`), `StepCount` and `LastCheckpointID` are reset but `TurnCheckpointIDs` is preserved (those checkpoints still need finalization). This is correct but non-obvious — a comment would prevent accidental "cleanup" later.

#### 3. Double redaction (minor perf)

`finalizeAllTurnCheckpoints` (`manual_commit_hooks.go:1638`) redacts the transcript, then `UpdateCommitted` (`committed.go:1079`) redacts again. Harmless but wastes CPU on large transcripts. Consider either skipping in the caller or adding a flag.

#### 4. `filesOverlapWithContent` skips deleted files silently (design question)

`content_overlap.go:99-106`: When a session-touched file is `git rm`'d, it's skipped (no overlap). If the session created a file and the user immediately deletes it, the commit won't get a checkpoint trailer. This may be intentional but isn't documented.

#### 5. Missing test: partial `HandleTurnEnd` failure

No test for e.g. 3 `TurnCheckpointIDs` where the 2nd `UpdateCommitted` fails. The code handles this correctly (continues processing, returns error count), but a test would lock that behavior.

#### 6. `TurnEndHandler` interface breaking change

`HandleTurnEnd` no longer takes `[]session.Action` (`strategy.go:469`). If any external forks implement this interface, they'll break. Internal-only should be fine.

---

### Test Quality

- All tests use `t.Parallel()` correctly (omitted only for `t.Setenv`/`t.Chdir`)
- Integration tests cover: basic finalization, carry-forward, multi-commit-per-turn, content overlap, reverted files, session reset, depleted sessions
- `content_overlap_test.go` has 12 unit tests covering all branches
- Low flakiness risk — all tests use `t.TempDir()` with no shared mutable state

---

### Summary

Well-executed simplification. The 1:1 model is easier to reason about, and the provisional-then-finalize pattern handles the timing gap cleanly. Suggestions are mostly about documenting subtle invariants (points 1, 2) and a missing edge-case test (point 5). No blocking issues.

  1571→//nolint:unparam // error return required by interface but hooks must return nil

  1584→			slog.Int("error_count", errCount),
  1585→		)
  1586→	}
  1587→	return nil
  1588→}

   202→	return dir

  1766→// carryForwardToNewShadowBranch creates a new shadow branch at the current HEAD
  1767→// containing the remaining uncommitted files and all session metadata.
  1768→// This enables the next commit to get its own unique checkpoint.
  1769→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) carryForwardToNewShadowBranch(
  1770→	logCtx context.Context,
  1771→	repo *git.Repository,
  1772→	state *SessionState,
  1773→	remainingFiles []string,
  1774→) {
  1775→	store := checkpoint.NewGitStore(repo)
  1776→
  1777→	// Don't include metadata directory in carry-forward. The carry-forward branch
  1778→	// only needs to preserve file content for comparison - not the transcript.
  1779→	// Including the transcript would cause sessionHasNewContent to always return true
  1780→	// because CheckpointTranscriptStart is reset to 0 for carry-forward.
  1781→	result, err := store.WriteTemporary(context.Background(), checkpoint.WriteTemporaryOptions{
  1782→		SessionID:         state.SessionID,
  1783→		BaseCommit:        state.BaseCommit,
  1784→		WorktreeID:        state.WorktreeID,
  1785→		ModifiedFiles:     remainingFiles,
  1786→		MetadataDir:       "",
  1787→		MetadataDirAbs:    "",
  1788→		CommitMessage:     "carry forward: uncommitted session files",
  1789→		IsFirstCheckpoint: false,
  1790→	})
  1791→	if err != nil {
  1792→		logging.Warn(logCtx, "post-commit: carry-forward failed",
  1793→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
  1794→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
  1795→		)
  1796→		return
  1797→	}
  1798→	if result.Skipped {
  1799→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "post-commit: carry-forward skipped (no changes)",
  1800→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
  1801→		)
  1802→		return
  1803→	}
  1804→
  1805→	// Update state for the carry-forward checkpoint.
  1806→	// CheckpointTranscriptStart = 0 is intentional: each checkpoint is self-contained with
  1807→	// the full transcript. This trades storage efficiency for simplicity:
  1808→	// - Pro: Each checkpoint is independently readable without needing to stitch together
  1809→	//   multiple checkpoints to understand the session history
  1810→	// - Con: For long sessions with multiple partial commits, each checkpoint includes
  1811→	//   the full transcript, which could be large
  1812→	// An alternative would be incremental checkpoints (only new content since last condensation),
  1813→	// but this would complicate checkpoint retrieval and require careful tracking of dependencies.
  1814→	state.FilesTouched = remainingFiles
  1815→	state.StepCount = 1
  1816→	state.CheckpointTranscriptStart = 0
  1817→	state.LastCheckpointID = ""
  1818→
  1819→	logging.Info(logCtx, "post-commit: carried forward remaining files",
  1820→		slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
  1821→		slog.Int("remaining_files", len(remainingFiles)),
  1822→	)
  1823→}
  1824→

Done. Here's what was added:

1. **Comment on `TurnCheckpointIDs` guard** (`manual_commit_hooks.go:641-645`) — Documents that the `state.Phase.IsActive()` check relies on the `ACTIVE + GitCommit → ACTIVE` state machine transition. If that ever changed, recording would silently break.

2. **Comment on carry-forward preserving `TurnCheckpointIDs`** (`manual_commit_hooks.go:1817-1819`) — Explains why `TurnCheckpointIDs` is intentionally not cleared when `StepCount`/`LastCheckpointID` are reset during carry-forward.

3. **`TestHandleTurnEnd_PartialFailure`** (`phase_postcommit_test.go`) — Sets up 3 `TurnCheckpointIDs` (2 real, 1 fake), calls `HandleTurnEnd`, and verifies: the 2 valid checkpoints are finalized with the full transcript, `TurnCheckpointIDs` is cleared, and no error is returned (best-effort).

Two bot reviews (Cursor and Copilot), both `COMMENTED` — no human reviews yet. Here's a summary:

**Cursor (3 comments):**
1. **Amend message drops checkpoint trailer** (`manual_commit_hooks.go:293`) — `PrepareCommitMsg` returns early when `sessionsWithContent` is empty, so amend flows may lose the trailer
2. **Finalization failures silently discard pending checkpoints** (`manual_commit_hooks.go:1630`) — `TurnCheckpointIDs` is cleared even when transcript loading fails
3. **Empty finalization data never replaces provisional content** (`committed.go:1087`) — `UpdateCommitted` skips writes when lengths are 0, so empty data can't clear provisional content

**Copilot (4 comments):**
1. **Missing redaction in UpdateCommitted** (`committed.go`) — prompts/context not redacted (though we just confirmed they are — line 1090/1104)
2. **Transcript redaction missing** (`committed.go`) — same concern for transcript (also already handled at line 1079)
3. **Can't clear prompt/context** (`committed.go`) — empty slices skip writes, so you can't intentionally clear them
4. **Missing error in log** (`manual_commit_hooks.go:1632`) — `os.ReadFile` failure log should include the error

Want me to look at any of these more closely? The Copilot redaction comments (#1, #2) appear to be false positives — `UpdateCommitted` does redact at lines 1079, 1090, and 1104.

   376→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) handleAmendCommitMsg(logCtx context.Context, commitMsgFile string) error {

   378→	content, err := os.ReadFile(commitMsgFile) //nolint:gosec // commitMsgFile is provided by git hook

> AGENT

OK, now I have full context on all 7 comments. Here's my assessment:

**Cursor comment 1 — "Amend message drops checkpoint trailer" (line 293):**
The concern is about `source="message"` amend flows. But looking at the code: amends use `source="commit"` (line 242) and are handled by `handleAmendCommitMsg` which preserves or restores the trailer. The `source="message"` path is for `git commit -m "..."` (non-amend). The early return at line 285 is correct — if no session has new content, there's nothing to link. **Not current / false positive.**

**Cursor comment 2 — "Finalization failures silently discard pending checkpoints" (line 1630):**
When transcript read fails, `TurnCheckpointIDs` is cleared and checkpoints keep their provisional transcripts. The concern is that clearing the IDs means we can never retry. But this is by design — the hook must not block, and the provisional data is already persisted. **By design, not a bug.** Though the comment could note the trade-off more explicitly.

**Cursor comment 3 — "Empty finalization data never replaces provisional content" (line 1087):**
The `len(opts.Transcript) > 0` guard means you can't clear a transcript by passing empty bytes. This is intentional — `UpdateCommitted` is for replacing with a *full* transcript, not clearing. **Not current / by design.**

**Copilot comments 1 & 2 — "Missing redaction":**
Both are **false positives**. `UpdateCommitted` redacts at lines 1080 (`redact.JSONLBytes`), 1091 (`redact.String`), and 1105 (`redact.Bytes`).

**Copilot comment 3 — "Can't clear prompt/context":**
Same as Cursor #3. **By design.**

**Copilot comment 4 — "Missing error in log" (line 1632):**
This one is **valid**. Line 1625 has `err` from `os.ReadFile` but line 1626-1628 doesn't include it in the log. Let me check:

Clearing \\`TurnCheckpointIDs\\` when the transcript is unreadable is intentional — the hook must not block, and the provisional transcript from PostCommit is already persisted on \\`entire/checkpoints/v1\\`. The checkpoints aren't lost, they just keep the partial transcript instead of the full one. Retrying isn't feasible since the transcript file may be gone by the next hook invocation.\"",
  "description": "Reply to Cursor comment 2"
}
